L2151: Figure 19 illustrates the proposed metabolic pathways of Bempedoic Acid (P: ETC-1002). The central molecule, P: ETC-1002, undergoes various metabolic transformations leading to metabolites M1 through M18.
Phase I Metabolism (M1, M2, M3, M4, M5, M6, M7, M8, M9, M10, M11, M12, M13, M15, M16, M17, M18):P: ETC-1002 is metabolized to M1 (via hydroxylation).
M1 is metabolized to M2 (via hydroxylation).
M2 is metabolized to M3 (via hydroxylation) and M4 (via hydroxylation).
M4 is metabolized to M7 (via hydroxylation).
M2 is also metabolized to M5 (via hydroxylation).
M5 is metabolized to M9 (via hydroxylation).
M3 is metabolized to M8 (via hydroxylation).
M8 is metabolized to M12 (via hydroxylation).
M12 is metabolized to M6 (via hydroxylation).
M6 is metabolized to M9 (via hydroxylation).
M10 is formed via hydroxylation of P: ETC-1002.
M13 is formed via hydroxylation of P: ETC-1002.
M16 is formed via hydroxylation of P: ETC-1002.
M17 is formed via hydroxylation of P: ETC-1002.
M18 is formed via hydroxylation of P: ETC-1002.

Phase II Metabolism (Glucuronide Conjugation):M1 is conjugated to Glucuronide to form M11.
M15 is formed via conjugation of M18 with Glucuronide.
L2152: Figure 20 is a flowchart illustrating the Putative Mechanism of Bempedoic Acid-Related Mortality. The mechanism involves several interconnected pathways leading to Hypoglycemia (severe/prolonged) and Morbidity/Death.
Initial Exposure: Repeated High-Dose Bempedoic Acid (AUC_{0-24} > 3000 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{hr}/\text{mL} (rat) or >5000 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{hr}/\text{mL} (monkey)) leads to High ETC-1002 (2X - 4X plasma).
Metabolic Pathways (Liver):
High ETC-1002 leads to ETC-1002-CoA.
ETC-1002-CoA is metabolized via CoASH to ACL Inhibition.
ACL Inhibition leads to ↓[Acetyl-CoA].
↓[Acetyl-CoA] leads to ↓[Malonyl-CoA] and ↑[Citrate] (transient).
↓[Malonyl-CoA] leads to ↑CPT 1.
↑CPT 1 leads to ↑Fatty Acid \beta-Oxidation.
↑Fatty Acid \beta-Oxidation leads to ↓Fatty Acid/Sterol Synthesis.
↓Fatty Acid/Sterol Synthesis leads to ↓Glycolysis (transient?) and ↓Gluconeogenesis.
↓Glycolysis (transient?) and ↓Gluconeogenesis both lead to ↓Glucose.
↓Glucose leads to ↓Glucose (plasma).
↓Glucose (plasma) leads to Hypoglycemia (severe/prolonged).
↓Glucose (plasma) also leads to ↓Glycogen.
↓Glycogen leads to ↓Glucose (plasma).
↑Fatty Acid \beta-Oxidation also leads to ↑Ketone Bodies (plasma), which is improved by ↑Vitamin B5.
Other Pathways:
Repeated High-Dose Bempedoic Acid also leads to ↓Food Consumption.
↓Food Consumption leads to ↑Peripheral Fatty Acid Mobilization.
↑Peripheral Fatty Acid Mobilization leads to ↑Fatty Acid \beta-Oxidation.
Outcome: Hypoglycemia (severe/prolonged) leads to Morbidity/Death. Exogenous Glucose Prevents Sequelae (Rescue Therapy) can prevent Morbidity/Death.
Legend:
Solid box = Direct Experimental Evidence
Dashed box = Published Evidence
Abbreviations:
ACL = ATP-Citrate Lyase
ACSVL1 = Very Long-Chain Acyl-CoA Synthetase 1
CPT-1 = Carnitine Palmitoyltransferase I

L2167: Figure 35 is a line graph showing the time course of LDL Cholesterol (mg/dL) over 52 weeks for patients receiving Bempedoic acid 180 mg or Placebo, stratified by Renal Impairment status (Normal and Mild vs. Moderate).
The Y-axis represents LDL Cholesterol (mg/dL), ranging from 60 to 180. The X-axis represents Week, ranging from 0 to 52.
Legend:
Treatment: Bempedoic acid 180 mg (Red line with circles) and Placebo (Teal line with circles).
Renal Impairment: Normal and Mild (Black circles) and Moderate (Black triangles).
Observed Trends:
Placebo Group: LDL Cholesterol levels remain stable, hovering around 100 mg/dL across all renal impairment groups (Normal and Mild, and Moderate).
Bempedoic acid 180 mg Group: LDL Cholesterol levels show a significant decrease from baseline (Week 0, approximately 100 mg/dL) to Week 4 (approximately 75 mg/dL). The levels then stabilize and remain significantly lower than the Placebo group throughout the 52-week period, generally ranging between 75 mg/dL and 85 mg/dL.
The graph demonstrates that Bempedoic acid 180 mg effectively lowers LDL Cholesterol compared to Placebo, and this effect is consistent across both Normal and Mild Renal Impairment and Moderate Renal Impairment groups.

L2173: Figure 41 is a schematic diagram illustrating the design of Trial 040, a 52-Week Double-Blind Treatment Period study.
The study begins with a Screening Period (Visit S1, Week -2) for High CV risk patients (HeFH or ASCVD) with hyperlipidemia on a stable maximally tolerated dose of statin with or without other stable modifying therapy. This leads to R (Randomization).
Following randomization, the study enters the 52-Week Double-Blind Treatment Period, divided into visits T1 through T7/EOS (End of Study).
The treatment groups are:
Bempedoic Acid 180 mg (n=1488)
Placebo (n=742)
Visit timeline (Weeks): S1 (-2), T1 (0), T2 (4), T3 (8), T4 (12), T5 (24), T6 (36), T7/EOS (52).

L2193: Figure 1A displays the Kaplan-Meier Survival Functions for Male Rats. The Y-axis represents Survival Probability (0.0 to 1.0) and the X-axis represents Survival time (week) (0 to 100). The legend indicates four dose groups:
0 mg/kg/day | Vehicle Control (Blue line)
3 mg/kg/day | Low (Red line)
10 mg/kg/day | Mid (Green line)
30 mg/kg/day | High (Brown line)
The survival curves for all groups start at 1.0 survival probability. The Vehicle Control group shows the highest survival probability over time, followed by the Low, Mid, and High dose groups. The High dose group shows the lowest survival probability, particularly after 80 weeks, where the survival probability drops below 0.8. The curves show stepwise decreases, indicating censoring events.
